\subsection{Equicontinuity and regional proximality}

\begin{leanrmk}
    Equicontinuity: \url{https://leanprover-community.github.io/mathlib4_docs/Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/Equicontinuity.html}

    Let's look in Mathlib for ``Equicontinuous family'' as in the definition below.
\end{leanrmk}

\begin{definition}[Equicontinuous family]
     Let $X$ and $Y$ be uniform spaces.  A set $S$ of continuous maps from $X$ to $Y$ is \emph{uniformly equicontinuous} if for every $\beta \subseteq Y^2$ in the uniform structure of $Y$ there is an $\alpha \subseteq X^2$ in the uniform structure of $X$ such that for all $f \in S$, $(f \otimes f)\alpha \subseteq \beta$.
\end{definition}

Note for the definition of equicontinuous system: A $G$-system $X$ for us is a compact Hausdorff space.  Compact Hausdorff spaces are uniform spaces with a unique uniformity given by the filter of all neighborhoods of the diagonal.

\begin{definition}[{Equicontinuous system, cf. 
\cite[Ch. 4, (2.1)]{devries_book_1993}}]
    \lean{isEquicontinuousSystem}
    \leanok{}
    A $S$-system $X$ is \emph{(uniformly) equicontinuous} if the set of maps $\{S: X \to X \mid g \in G\}$ is a (uniformly) equicontinuous family.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}
    \label{lemma_arbitrary_product_of_equi_is_equi}
    \lean{prodOfEquiSystemsIsEqui}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup.  The arbitrary product of equicontinuous $S$-systems is an equicontinuous $S$-system.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lemma_product_of_equi_is_equi}
    \dgg{Hopefully just follow the proof of \cref{lemma_product_of_equi_is_equi}, copied here:} If $X$ and $Y$ are $S$-systems, then $X \times Y$ is an $S$-system via the diagonal action.  We need to show that the family of maps $\{(x,y) \mapsto (sx,sy) : s \in S\}$ from $X \times Y$ to $X \times Y$ is equicontinuous. Let $\beta \subseteq (X \times Y)^2$ be an entourage.  By some general fact concerning the uniform structure on a Cartesian product of uniform spaces (in mathlib, see \verb|entourageProd_subset|), there exist entourages $\beta_X \subseteq X^2$ and $\beta_Y \subseteq Y^2$ such that $\beta_X \times \beta_Y \subseteq \beta$. By equicontinuity of the systems $X$ and $Y$, there exist entourages $\alpha_X \subseteq X^2$ and $\alpha_Y \subseteq Y^2$ such that for all $s \in S$, $(s \otimes s) \alpha_X \subseteq \beta_X$ and $(s \otimes s) \alpha_Y \subseteq \beta_Y$. Then $
    \alpha \defeq \alpha_X \times \alpha_Y$ is an entourage of $X \times Y$, and for all $s \in S$, $(s \otimes s \otimes s \otimes s)\alpha \subseteq \beta$, as desired.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}
    \label{lemma_subsys_of_equi_is_equi}
    \lean{subsystemOfEquicontinuousIsEquicontinuous}
    \leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup, and let $X$ be an equicontinuous $S$-system.  All subsystems of $X$ are equicontinuous $S$-systems.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok{}
    By assumption, the family of maps indexed by $S$ is equicontinuous.  Let $Z$ be a subsystem of $X$.  By definition, the system $Z$ is equicontinuous if the family of maps $\{\restr{s}{Z}: Z \to Z | s \in S\}$ is equicontinuous.  \dgg{This should be a general topological fact.}
\end{proof}


\begin{theorem}
    \label{thm_isom_to_equi_is_equi}
    \lean{systemIsEquiIfIsomToEqui}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup and $X$ and $Y$ be $S$-systems.  If $X$ is isomorphic to $Y$ and $Y$ is equicontinuous, then $X$ is equicontinuous.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
    \dgg{Should be simple.}
\end{proof}


\begin{definition}[{Equicontinuous ICER, cf. \cite{ellis_ellis_book_2014}}]
\lean{isEquicontinuousICER}
\leanok{}
    Let $X$ be a $G$-system.  An ICER $R$ of $X$ is \emph{equicontinuous} if the quotient system (cf. \cref{def_quotient_system}) $X/R$ is equicontinuous.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[{cf. \cite[pg. 283]{devries_book_1993}}]
    \label{lemma_rp_and_equicontinuity}
    \lean{equicontinuousIffRPTrivialIfSurjective}
    \leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup. An $S$-system $X$ on which $S$ acts by surjections is equicontinuous if and only if $\RP \subseteq \Delta$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
    \dgg{We need a proof of this.}
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[{cf. \cite[pg. 283]{devries_book_1993}}]
    \label{lemma_rp_and_equicontinuity}
    \lean{equicontinuousIffRPMTrivial}
    \leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup. An $S$-system $X$ is equicontinuous if and only if $\RP^- \subseteq \Delta$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok{}
    Suppose $\RP^- \subseteq \Delta$.  To see that $X$ is equicontinuous, let $\alpha \subseteq X^2$ be an entourage.  Since
    \[
        \bigcap_{\gamma} \overline{S \gamma} = \RP^- \subseteq \Delta \subseteq \alpha,
    \]
    by a general topological fact (\emph{if $\mathcal{K}$ is a collection of closed sets, $U$ is open, and $\cap_{Z \in \mathcal{K}} Z \subseteq U$, then there exists a finite subcollection $\mathcal{K}' \subseteq \mathcal{K}$ such that $\cap_{Z \in \mathcal{K}'} Z \subseteq U$}; to get this in mathlib, use \verb|IsCompact.elim_finite_subfamily_closed|) there exists an entourage $\beta \subseteq X^2$ such that $\overline{S \beta} \subseteq \alpha$.  Thus, $S \beta \subseteq \alpha$, as was to be shown.

    Conversely, suppose $X$ is an equicontinuous $S$-system.
    We will show that if $(x,y) \not\in \Delta$, then $(x,y) \not\in \RP^-$. Suppose $x \neq y$.  Choose $\beta \subseteq X^2$ an entourage such that $(x,y) \not\in \overline{\beta}$.  Since $X$ is equicontinuous, there exists $\alpha \subseteq X^2$ entourage such that $G \alpha \subseteq \beta$.  Thus, $(x,y) \not\in \overline{G \alpha}$, so $(x,y) \not\in \RP^-$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}
    \label{lemma_rp_characterization_of_equicont}
    \lean{minimalFactorEquicontinuousIffRPInFactorRelation}
    \leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a commutative semigroup and $X$ be a minimal $S$-system.  A factor $\pi: X \to Y$ is equicontinuous if and only if $\RP \subseteq R_\pi$.  The same holds with $\RP$ replaced by $\RP^-$.
\end{lemma}
    %\dgg{We might only get: if a factor $\pi: X \to Y$ is equicontinuous, then $\RP \subseteq R_\pi$.}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lemma_rp_functorial_property_minimal,lemma_rp_and_equicontinuity,thm_forward_equals_backward_rp}
    Note that by the definition of $R_\pi$, $\RP \subseteq R_\pi$ if and only if $(\pi \times \pi) \RP \subseteq \Delta_Y$.  By \cref{lemma_rp_functorial_property_minimal}, this is if and only if $\RP_Y \subseteq \Delta_Y$.  By \cref{lemma_rp_and_equicontinuity}, this is if and only if $Y$ is equicontinuous.

    That the same statement holds with $\RP$ replaced by $\RP^-$ follows since $\RP = \RP^-$ by \cref{thm_forward_equals_backward_rp}.
\end{proof}
    %\dgg{Alt: Suppose the factor $\pi$ is equicontinuous so that $\RP_Y \subseteq \Delta$. By \cref{lemma_rp_functorial_property}, $(\pi \times \pi) \RP \subseteq \RP_Y \subseteq \Delta$. By the definition of $R_\pi$, $(\pi \times \pi) \RP \subseteq \Delta_Y$ implies $\RP \subseteq R_\pi$, as desired.} 

\begin{lemma}
    \label{lemma_rp_characterization_of_equicont_icer}
    \lean{minimalICEREquicontinuousIffRPInICER}
    \leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a commutative semigroup and $X$ be a minimal $S$-system.  An ICER $Q$ of $X$ is equicontinuous if and only if $\RP \subseteq Q$. The same holds with $\RP$ replaced by $\RP^-$.
\end{lemma}
%\dgg{We might only get: if $Q$ is an equicontinuous ICER, then $\RP \subseteq Q$.}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lemma_rp_characterization_of_equicont,thm_forward_equals_backward_rp}
    Suppose $Q$ is an ICER.  By definition, it is equicontinuous if and only if $\pi_Q: (X,T) \to (X/Q,T)$ is equicontinuous.  By \cref{lemma_rp_characterization_of_equicont}, this happens if and only if $\RP \subseteq R_{\pi_Q} = Q$.

    That the same statement holds with $\RP$ replaced by $\RP^-$ follows since $\RP = \RP^-$ by \cref{thm_forward_equals_backward_rp}.
\end{proof}
    %\dgg{Alt: Suppose $Q$ is an equicontinuous ICER.  By definition, $\pi_Q: (X,T) \to (X/Q,T)$ is equicontinuous.  By \cref{lemma_rp_characterization_of_equicont}, this implies $\RP \subseteq R_{\pi_Q} = Q$.}

\subsubsection{Unused from this subsection}

\begin{lemma}
    \label{lemma_product_of_equi_is_equi}
    \lean{diagSystemOfEquiSystemsIsEquiSystem}
    \leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup.  The product of equicontinuous $S$-systems is an equicontinuous $S$-system.
    \dgg{Deprecated in favor of \cref{lemma_arbitrary_product_of_equi_is_equi}}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok{}
    
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}
    \label{lemma_minimal_equi_implies_homogeneous}
    Let $S$ be a commutative semigroup, and let $X$ be a minimal, equicontinuous $S$-system.  For all $x, y \in X$, there exists a factor map $\varphi: X \to X$ of $S$-systems such that $\varphi x = y$.
\end{lemma}

\dgg{If it would help here, we can prove first that a minimal action by a commutative semigroup acts by homeomorphisms.   (Update: we have this in the RP paper.) Then perhaps in \cref{lemma_minimal_equi_implies_homogeneous} we could get that $\varphi$ is a homeomorphism, hence an automorphism of the $S$-system $X$.}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch.]
\uses{lemma_img_of_inv_is_inv}
    Let $x, y \in X$.  By minimality, exists a net $(s_n)_n \subseteq S$ such that $\lim_n s_n x = y$.  Then, by commutativity and the fact that $S$ acts by continuous maps, for all $t \in S$, $\lim_n s_n tx = ty$.  Thus, for all $z \in Sx$, $\lim_n s_n z$ exists.

    We claim that the map $f: Sx \to X$ defined by $f(z) = \lim_n s_n z$ is uniformly continuous. Indeed, let $\alpha \subseteq X^2$ be an entourage.  We need to show that there exists an entourage $\beta \subseteq (Sx)^2$ such that $f \beta \subseteq \alpha$.  By equicontinuity of the action, there exists $\beta \subseteq X^2$ such that $\overline{S \beta} \subseteq \alpha$. For $(w,z) \in \beta$, we see that
    \[f(w,z) = (f(w),f(z)) = (\lim_n s_n w, \lim_n s_n z) = \lim_n s_n (w,z) \in \overline{S\beta} \subseteq \alpha,\]
    as was to be shown.

    Since $f: Sx \to X$ is uniformly continuous and, by minimality, $Sx$ is dense in $X$, we have that $f$ extends uniquely to a continuous function $\varphi: X \to X$.

    We claim that $\varphi$ is $S$-equivariant.  Indeed, let $t \in S$.  Note that for all $z \in Sx$, by the definition of $f$ and the fact that action by $t$ is continuous, $f(tz) = t f(z)$.  So, $\varphi \circ t = t \circ \varphi$ on a dense set, and hence on all of $X$ \dgg{this is fuzzy}.
    
    We claim $\varphi x = y$. Indeed, note that for all $m$, since $s_m x \in Sx$, $\varphi(s_m x) = f(s_m x) = \lim_{n} s_n s_m x = s_m \lim_n s_n x = s_m y$.  Then
    \[\varphi(y) = \varphi(\lim_m s_m x) = \lim_m \varphi(s_m x) = \lim_m s_m \varphi (x) = \]
    \dgg{This is not complete.}
    
    By \cref{lemma_img_of_inv_is_inv}, $\varphi X$ is a subsystem of $X$.  By minimality, $\varphi X = X$.  Therefore, $\varphi: X \to X$ is a factor map of $S$-systems.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Distality, equicontinuity, and actions by homeomorphisms}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lemma_equi_implies_distal}
\lean{equicontinuousMinCommSystemsAreDistal}
    Let $S$ be a commutative semigroup and $X$ be a minimal $S$-system.  If $X$ is equicontinuous, then $X$ is distal.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lemma_dense_S_minimal_points_in_X2,lemma_thick_right_shift_is_thick,lemma_synd_thick_dual}
    Suppose for a contradiction that $X$ is equicontinuous but that there exist distinct and proximal points $x, y \in X$.
    
    Let $\alpha \subseteq X^2$ be a neighborhood of the diagonal such that $(x,y) \in X^2 \setminus \overline{\alpha}$.  By the equicontinuity assumption, there exists a neighborhood of the diagonal $\beta \subseteq X^2$ such that $S \beta \subseteq \alpha$.
    
    Since $x$ and $y$ are proximal, $S(x,y) \cap \beta \neq \emptyset$.  Therefore, there exists $s \in S$ such that $s(x,y) \in \beta$, and we have that
    \[(x,y) \in s^{-1} \beta \cap (X^2 \setminus \overline{\alpha}).\]
    By \cref{lemma_dense_S_minimal_points_in_X2}, the set of points in $X^2$ that are $S$-uniformly recurrent is dense. Choose
    \[(x',y') \in s^{-1} \beta \cap (X^2 \setminus \overline{\alpha})\]
    so that $(x',y')$ is $S$-uniformly recurrent.  We have by the definition of uniform recurrence that the set
    \[R\big((x',y'), s^{-1} \beta \cap (X^2 \setminus \overline{\alpha})\big)\]
    is syndetic.

    On the other hand, since $Ss(x',y') \subseteq  S\beta \subseteq \alpha$, we have that the set $R((x',y'), \alpha)$ contains $Ss$, and, hence, by \cref{lemma_thick_right_shift_is_thick}, is thick.  This is a contradiction since $s^{-1} \beta \cap (X^2 \setminus \overline{\alpha})$ and $\alpha$ are disjoint while, by \cref{lemma_synd_thick_dual}, thick sets and syndetic sets have non-empty intersection.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lemma_distal_implies_homeo}
\lean{semigroupActsByHomeosOnMinCommDistalSystems}
    Let $S$ be a commutative semigroup and $X$ be a minimal $S$-system.  If $X$ is distal, then $S$ acts by homeomorphisms.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lemma_minimal_comm_action_implies_surjective}
    We know already by \cref{lemma_minimal_comm_action_implies_surjective} that $S$ acts by surjections, so we only need to show that $S$ acts by injective maps and the inverse maps are continuous. Let $x, y \in X$ and $s \in S$.  If $sx = sy$, then $x$ and $y$ are proximal.  Since $X$ is distal, $x = y$, whereby $s$ is injective. 

    For each $s \in S$, to show the inverse map $s^{-1} : X \to X$ is continuous, let $U \subseteq X$ be an open set. Because $s$ is bijective, $s (U) = s (U^c)^c.$
    Since $U^c$ is compact, $s(U^c)$ is compact, hence closed. Therefore, $s(U)$ is open, as was to be shown.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The equicontinuous structure relation}

\begin{definition}[{cf. \cite[Ch. V, (1.3)]{devries_book_1993}}]
\label{def_equicontinuous_structure_relation}
\lean{equiStructureRelation}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup and $X$ be an $S$-system.  The \emph{equicontinuous structure relation} on $X$, denoted $\seq(X)$ (or just $\seq$ if $X$ is understood), is the intersection of all equicontinuous ICERS on $X$.  (Note that $X^2$ is always one such ICER, so the intersection is non-empty.)
\end{definition}



\begin{definition}
    \label{def_largest_equi_factor}
    \lean{isLargestEquicontinuousFactor}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup and $X$ be an $S$-system.  An $S$-system $Y$ is the \emph{largest equicontinuous factor} of $X$ if
    \begin{itemize}
        \item $Y$ is a factor of $X$;
        \item $Y$ is an equicontinuous $S$-system; and
        \item for all equicontinuous factors $Z$ of $X$, $Z$ is a factor of $Y$.
    \end{itemize}
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lemma_seq_is_equi_and_largest}
\lean{equiStructureRelationIsLargestEquiICER}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup and $X$ be an $S$-system.  The equicontinuous structure relation $\seq$ is an equicontinuous ICER, and $X \to X/\seq$ is the largest equicontinuous factor of $X$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{def_isomorphism,thm_system_is_isomorphic_to_injective_factor_image,lemma_arbitrary_product_of_equi_is_equi,lemma_subsys_of_equi_is_equi,thm_isom_to_equi_is_equi,def_largest_equi_factor}
    Let $(R_i)_{i \in I}$ be the collection of equicontinuous ICERs of $X$, so that $\seq = \bigcap_{i \in I} R_i$.  The map
    $\varphi: X / \seq \to \prod_{i \in I} X/R_i$ defined by $[x]_{\seq} \mapsto \prod_{i \in I} [x]_{R_i}$ is continuous, injective, and intertwines the actions of $S$, so $X/\seq$ is isomorphic as an $S$-system (\cref{def_isomorphism}) to its image (by \cref{thm_system_is_isomorphic_to_injective_factor_image}), a subsystem of $\prod_{i \in I} X/R_i$.  Since products (\cref{lemma_arbitrary_product_of_equi_is_equi}) and subsystems (\cref{lemma_subsys_of_equi_is_equi}) of equicontinuous systems are equicontinuous, we have that $X / \seq$ is equicontinuous (by \cref{thm_isom_to_equi_is_equi}).

    To see that $X \to X / \seq$ is the largest equicontinuous factor (\cref{def_largest_equi_factor}), note that we have already discussed above that $X / \seq$ is equicontinuous and is a factor of $X$.  Note then that if $\pi : X \to Y$ is an equicontinuous factor, then by the definition of $\seq$, $R_\pi$ contains $\seq$.  The quotient $X / \seq \to X / R_\pi$ is a factor map of $S$-systems.  But $X / R_\pi$ is isomorphic to $Y$, hence $Y$ is a factor of $X/\seq$, as was to be shown.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Unused from this subsection}

\begin{theorem}
\label{thm_equi_structure_relation_is_equi_ICER}
\lean{equiStructureRelationIsEquiICER}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a commutative semigroup and $X$ be a minimal $S$-system.  The equicontinuous structure relation is an equicontinuous ICER.
    \dgg{Deprecated in favor of \cref{lemma_seq_is_equi_and_largest}}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lemma_intersection_of_icers,lemma_rp_characterization_of_equicont_icer}
\leanok{}
    The equicontinuous structure relation $\seq$ is an ICER by \cref{lemma_intersection_of_icers}.

    To show that it is an equicontinuous ICER, it suffices by \cref{lemma_rp_characterization_of_equicont_icer} to show that $\RP \subseteq \seq$.
    
    Let $R$ be an equicontinuous ICER.  We must show that $\RP \subseteq R$.  This follows by \cref{lemma_rp_characterization_of_equicont_icer}.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}
\label{def_kronecker_factor}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup and $X$ be an $S$-system.  The Kronecker factor of $X$ is the quotient system $X/\seq$ together with the quotient map $\pi: X \to X/\seq$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}
    \label{thm_kronecker_is_equicontinuous_system}
    Let $S$ be a commutative semigroup and $X$ be a minimal $S$-system. The Kronecker factor of $X$ is an equicontinuous system.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\uses{thm_equi_structure_relation_is_equi_ICER}
    Should follow immediately from \cref{thm_equi_structure_relation_is_equi_ICER} and the definition of Kronecker factor.
\end{proof}

\dgg{Do we use the following theorem anywhere?  Will wait to build into Lean.}

\begin{theorem}
\label{thm_kronecker_factor_description}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup and $X$ be an $S$-system.  Let $\pi: X \to K$ be the Kronecker factor.  For all factors $\sigma: X \to Z$ of $S$-systems with $Z$ equicontinuous, there exists a continuous surjection $\xi: K \to Z$ such that $\sigma = \xi \circ \pi$.  In particular, the Kronecker factor is the largest equicontinuous factor of $X$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lemma_factors_and_icers}
    Since $\sigma: X \to Z$ is equicontinuous, $R_\sigma$ is an equicontinuous ICER.  By the definition of $\seq$, $\seq \subseteq R_\sigma$.  It follows then by \cref{lemma_factors_and_icers} that there exists a continuous surjection $\xi: K \to Z$ such that $\sigma = \xi \circ \pi$.
\end{proof}

\section{Ultrafilters}
\label{sec_ultrafilters}

Needs: \cref{sec_topological_dynamical_systems}

Contains:
\begin{itemize}
    \item Topological semigroups

    \item Ultrafilters as dynamical objects
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Topological semigroups}

\dgg{Worth noting that in the Hindman-Strauss book, they assume Hausdorff implicitly.  So, really they treat compact, Hausdorff right-topological semigroups.}

\begin{definition}[{Right-topological semigroup, cf. \cite[Def. 2.1]{hindman_strauss_book_2012}}]
    A right-topological semigroup is a topological semigroup $S$ such that for all $s \in S$, $\rho_s: S \to S$ is continuous.
\end{definition}

\begin{leanrmk}
    We likely do not need to define ``right topological semigroup.''  Any statements regarding these objects can be make the necessary assumptions, eg:
    \begin{center}
        \verb|{X}[Semigroup S][TopologicalSpace X][CompactSpace X]| \\ \verb|[r_cts : \forall (s : S), continuous fun (x : S) => x * s]|
    \end{center}

    There are useful results here: \url{https://leanprover-community.github.io/mathlib4_docs/Mathlib/Topology/Algebra/Semigroup.html}
    \begin{itemize}
        \item \verb|exists_idempotent_of_compact_t2_of_continuous_mul_left| is a theorem that shows that a compact, right topological semigroup contains an idempotent

        \item \verb|exists_idempotent_in_compact_subsemigroup| proves the same thing but for a compact subsemigroup.
    \end{itemize}    
\end{leanrmk}


\begin{theorem}[{\cite[Thm 2.5]{hindman_strauss_book_2012}}]
\lean{compactT2RTSemigroupContainsIdempotent}
\leanok{}
    A compact, Hausdorff right topological semigroup contains an idempotent.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok{}
    This is \verb|exists_idempotent_of_compact_t2_of_continuous_mul_left|.
\end{proof}

For all the following results, cf. \cite[Corollary 2.6 and surrounding]{hindman_strauss_book_2012}. \dgg{There is some going back and forth between compact and closed in these results.  These are equivalent for Hausdorff spaces.  Probably just choose the formulation that makes applying the lemma easiest.}

\begin{lemma}[Principal ideals are closed]
\label{lemma_principal_ideal_is_compact}
\lean{principalLeftIdealClosed}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a compact, Hausdorff right-topological semigroup, and let $t \in S$.  The set $St$ is closed.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok{}
    Since $S$ is compact and $\rho_t$ is continuous, $\rho_t(S) = St$ is compact.  Since $S$ is Hausdorff, $St$ is closed.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Left ideal contains a closed left ideal]
\label{lemma_left_ideal_contains_closed_left_ideal}
\lean{leftIdealContainsClosedLeftIdeal}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a compact, Hausdorff right-topological semigroup.  Every left ideal contains a closed left ideal.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lemma_principal_ideal_is_compact}
\leanok{}
    Let $L$ be a left ideal.  Let $t \in L$.  Since $L$ is  a left ideal, $St$ is a left ideal contained in $L$.  By \cref{lemma_principal_ideal_is_compact}, $St$ is closed.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Minimal left ideals are compact]
\label{lemma_minimal_left_ideal_compact}
\lean{minimalLeftIdealCompact}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a compact, right-topological semigroup.  Minimal left ideals of $S$ are compact.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lemma_principal_ideal_is_compact}
\leanok{}
    Let $L$ be a minimal left ideal of $S$.  Let $t \in L$. Then $St$ is a left ideal of $S$ contained in $L$.  Since $L$ is minimal, $St = L$.  By \cref{lemma_principal_ideal_is_compact}, $St$ is compact.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}
\label{thm_every_left_contains_minimal_left}
\lean{leftIdealContainsMinLeftIdeal}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a compact, Hausdorff right-topological semigroup.  Every left ideal of $S$ contains a minimal left ideal.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lemma_principal_ideal_is_compact,lemma_intersection_of_ideals_is_ideal}
\leanok{}
    Let $L$ be a left ideal of $S$.  Consider the collection $\mathcal{C}$ of compact left ideals of $S$ contained in $L$.  For $s \in L$, we see by \cref{lemma_principal_ideal_is_compact} that $Ss \subseteq L$ is a compact left ideal of $S$ contained in $L$, so $\mathcal{C}$ is non-empty.  Any totally ordered chain in $\mathcal{C}$ has a lower bound in $\mathcal{C}$, namely the intersection, which is non-empty and compact since $S$ is Hausdorff, and which is an ideal by \cref{lemma_intersection_of_ideals_is_ideal}.  By Zorn's lemma, $\mathcal{C}$ has a minimal element.  This is a minimal left ideal of $S$ contained in $L$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lemma_exists_minimal_left_ideal}
\lean{rightTopSemigroupContainsMinLeftIdeal}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a compact, Hausdorff right-topological semigroup.  The semigroup $S$ contains a minimal left ideal.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{thm_every_left_contains_minimal_left}
\leanok{}
    The semigroup $S$ is a left ideal of $S$, so by \cref{thm_every_left_contains_minimal_left}, $S$ contains a minimal left ideal of $S$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}
    \label{exists_idempotent_in_compact_subsemigroup}
    \lean{compactSubsemigroupContainsIdempotent}
    \leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a compact, Hausdorff right-topological semigroup.  Every non-empty, compact subsemigroup of $S$ contains an idempotent.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok{}
    This is precisely  \verb|exists_idempotent_in_compact_subsemigroup| in mathlib.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lemma_left_ideal_contains_idempotent}
\lean{leftIdealContainsIdempotent}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a compact, Hausdorff right-topological semigroup.  Every left ideal of $S$ contains an idempotent element.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lemma_exists_minimal_left_ideal,lemma_minimal_left_ideal_compact,exists_idempotent_in_compact_subsemigroup}
\leanok{}
    Every left ideal contains a minimal left ideal (\cref{lemma_exists_minimal_left_ideal}).  Minimal left ideals are compact (\cref{lemma_minimal_left_ideal_compact}) subsemigroups.  Apply \cref{exists_idempotent_in_compact_subsemigroup}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Ultrafilters}

\subsection{Ultrafilters as a set}

\begin{leanrmk}
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item Ultrafilters: \url{https://leanprover-community.github.io/mathlib4_docs/Mathlib/Order/Filter/Ultrafilter/Defs.html}
    
        \item \verb|Ultrafilter.instPure| is the principal ultrafilter associated to a point.  This allows us to see $S$ as a subset of \verb|Ultrafilter S|.  \verb|pure s| is the principal ultrafilter at $s$
    \end{enumerate}
\end{leanrmk}

\begin{leanrmk}
    The type for ultrafilters on $S$ is the structure \verb|Ultrafilter S|.
\end{leanrmk}

\begin{definition}[Ultrafilter]
    Let $S$ be a set.  An ultrafilter on $S$ is a maximal filter on $S$ by containment. 
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Set of ultrafilters]
    Let $S$ be a set.  The set of ultrafilters on $S$ is denoted $\beta S$.
\end{definition}



\begin{definition}[Principal ultrafilter at $s$]
    Let $S$ be a set.  The principal ultrafilter at $s$ is denoted $\delta_s$.
\end{definition}

\subsection{Ultrafilters as a topological space}

\begin{leanrmk}
    A realization of the Stone-\Cech{} compactification as the space of ultrafilters: \url{https://leanprover-community.github.io/mathlib4_docs/Mathlib/Topology/Compactification/StoneCech.html}
\end{leanrmk}

\begin{definition}[Topology on $\beta S$]
\label{def_cmpt_hausdorff_top_on_ultra}
\mathlibok
    There is a compact, Hausdorff topology on $\beta S$ in which $S$ (realized as the set of principal ultrafilters on $S$) is a dense subset.  
\end{definition}

\begin{leanrmk}
    The topology is accessible in Lean as \verb|[TopologicalSpace (Ultrafilter S)]|.  This topology makes $\beta S$ the Stone-Cech compactification of $S$, though we don't need to know that.  This is in mathlib as \verb|ultrafilter_compact| and \verb|Ultrafilter.t2Space|
\end{leanrmk}

\begin{theorem}
\label{thm_cts_extend}
\mathlibok
    Let $S$ be a semigroup, $X$ be a compact, Hausdorff space, and $f: S \to X$.  There is a unique continuous function $\tilde f: \beta S \to X$ that extends $f$, in the sense that $\tilde f (\delta_s) = f(s)$ for all $s \in S$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{leanrmk}
    The extension of $f: S \to X$ to $f: \beta S \to X$ is accomplished in Lean via \verb|Ultrafilter.extend|.
\end{leanrmk}

% \begin{definition}[Stone-\Cech{} compactification]
%     The Stone-\Cech{} compactification of a discrete semigroup $S$, denoted $\beta S$, is a compact Hausdorff space with the universal property: for all maps $S \to X$ into a compact Hausdorff space, there exists a unique continuous map $\beta S \to X$ so that the diagram (with the canonical injection $S \to \beta G$) commutes.  It is also a compact right topological semigroup.
% \end{definition}

The following is an important characterization of ultrafilters that belong to the closure of a subset of $S$.
\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:characterization_mem_closure_pure}
\lean{memClosurePureIff}
\leanok{}
Let $S$ be a semigroup, $B \subseteq S$ and $p \in \beta S$. Then $p \in \overline{B}$ if and only if $B \in p$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok{}
\end{proof}

\subsection{Ultrafilters as a semigroup}

\begin{leanrmk}
    A proof of Hindman's theorem via idempotent ultrafilters: \url{https://leanprover-community.github.io/mathlib4_docs/Mathlib/Combinatorics/Hindman.html}
\end{leanrmk}

\begin{definition}[Semigroup structure on $\beta S$]
\label{def_ultrafilter_multiplication}
\mathlibok
    Let $S$ be a semigroup.  For $p, q \in \beta S$, define
    \[pq = \{A \subseteq S : \{s \in S : \{t \in S : st \in A\} \in q \} \in p \}.\]
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Left/right multiplication maps]
Let $S$ be a semigroup and $s \in S$.  Define $\lambda_s, \rho_s: \beta S \to \beta S$ to be left and right multiplication by $s$.
\end{definition}

\begin{leanrmk}
    This product makes $\beta S$ into a semigroup.  In Mathlib, the semigroup structure is provided in \verb|Ultrafilter.semigroup| in the Hindman's theorem file (link above).  Once we fix an instance of it, the typeclass search will recognize \verb|[Semigroup (Ultrafilter S)]|.

    Of note: the multiplication is defined in a very short and quite confusing way.  But it is not hard to prove \cref{def_ultrafilter_multiplication}.
\end{leanrmk}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lemma_ultrafilter_mult_with_one_principal}
\lean{membershipInLeftMultByPrincipal}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup, $s \in S$, $B \subseteq S$, and $p \in \beta S$. Then $B \in \delta_s p$ if and only if $s^{-1} B \in p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok{}
    Each statement is equivalent with the one following it.
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item $B \in \delta_s p$
        \item $\{a \in S : \{t \in S : at \in B \} \in p \} \in \delta_s$
        \item $\{t \in S : st \in B \} \in p$
        \item $\{t \in S : t \in s^{-1}B \} \in p$
        \item $s^{-1}B \in p$
    \end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lemma_pull_out_inverse_mult_in_basis_set}
\lean{preimageClosureDescription}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup, $s \in S$, and $B \subseteq S$. Then $\overline{s^{-1} B} = \delta_s^{-1} \overline{B}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lem:characterization_mem_closure_pure,lemma_ultrafilter_mult_with_one_principal}
\leanok{}
    Each line is equivalent to the one following it.
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item $p \in \overline{s^{-1} B}$
        \item $s^{-1} B \in p$ (by \cref{lem:characterization_mem_closure_pure})
        \begin{itemize}
            \item By \cref{lemma_ultrafilter_mult_with_one_principal}
        \end{itemize}
        \item $B \in \delta_s p$
        \item $\delta_s p \in \overline{B}$
        \item $p \in \delta_s^{-1} \overline{B}$
    \end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}
    \label{lemma_ru_is_r_for_idempotent}
    \lean{minimalIdempotentsAreLeftIdentites}
    \leanok{}
    Let $L \subseteq \beta S$ be a minimal left ideal, and let $u \in L$  be idempotent.  For all $p \in L$, $pu = p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok{}
    Let $p \in L$.  Note that $Lu$ is a left ideal contained in $L$.  By minimality, $Lu = L$.  Therefore, there exists $q \in L$ such that $p = qu$.  Then $pu = quu = qu = p$, as was to be shown.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Minimal ultrafilter]
\label{def_minimal_ultrafilter}
\lean{isMinimalUltrafilter}
\leanok{}
An ultrafilter is \emph{minimal} if it belongs to a minimal left ideal of $\beta S$.
\end{definition}

\dgg{Hindman-Strauss Def. 1.37 define ``minimal idempotent'' as an idempotent minimal with respect to an ordering.  Then, they prove immediately that this is the same (at least in $\beta S$, in which every minimal left ideal has an idempotent) as belonging to a minimal left ideal.}

% \end{lemma}

\subsection{Ultrafilters as a topological semigroup}


\begin{theorem}[{cf. \cite[Thms 4.1 and 4.4, pg. 88]{hindman_strauss_book_2012}}]
\label{thm_ultra_right_mult_continuous}
\lean{rightMultUltraContinuous}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup.  For all $p \in \beta S$, right multiplication by $p$ as a map $\beta S \to \beta S$ is continuous.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
    Proof that multiplication on the right by a fixed ultrafilter is continuous is \verb|Ultrafilter.continuous_mul_left| in the Hindman file (this matches with Hindman-Strauss book: a compact, right-topological semigroup (Hindman-Strauss, Def. 2.1), meaning multiplication on the right by a fixed element of $\beta G$ is continuous (Hindman-Strauss, Theorems 4.1 and 4.4: $\beta S$ is a compact, right topological semigroup)).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[{cf. \cite[Thms 4.1 and 4.4, pg. 88]{hindman_strauss_book_2012}}]
\label{lemma_left_multiplication_is_continuous}
\lean{leftMultPrincipalUltraContinuous}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup.  For all $s \in S$, left multiplication by the principal ultrafilter at $s$ as a map $\beta S \to \beta S$ is continuous.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lemma_pull_out_inverse_mult_in_basis_set}
\leanok{}
    Denote left multiplication by $s$ by $\lambda_s: \beta S \to \beta S$.  It follows from \cref{lemma_pull_out_inverse_mult_in_basis_set} that for all $A \subseteq S$,
    \[\lambda_s^{-1} \overline{A} = \overline{s^{-1} A}.\]
    This suffices to show that $\lambda_s$ is continuous by the definition of the topology on $S$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}
\label{thm_ultrafilters_are_compact_right_top}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup.  $\beta S$ is a compact, Hausdorff right-topological semigroup
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\uses{thm_ultra_right_mult_continuous}
    That $\beta S$ is compact, Hausdorff is established, already in Lean, too.  That right multiplication is continuous is also established: see \cref{thm_ultra_right_mult_continuous} above.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lemma_exists_idempotent_in_left_ideals}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup.  Every left ideal in $\beta S$ contains a idempotent.
\end{lemma}

\dgg{Wait to formulate in lean.  May just be able to quote \cref{lemma_left_ideal_contains_idempotent} and rely on typeclass inference to pick up the hypotheses.}

\begin{proof}
\uses{thm_ultrafilters_are_compact_right_top,lemma_left_ideal_contains_idempotent}
    From \cref{thm_ultrafilters_are_compact_right_top}, $\beta S$ is a compact, Hausdorff right-topological semigroup.  By \cref{lemma_left_ideal_contains_idempotent}, every left ideal contains an idempotent.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lemma_exists_minimal_idempotent_in_left_ideals}
\lean{leftIdealInBetaSContainsMinIdempotent}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup.  Every left ideal in $\beta S$ contains a minimal idempotent.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{thm_ultrafilters_are_compact_right_top,lemma_exists_minimal_left_ideal,lemma_left_ideal_contains_idempotent}
\leanok{}
    From \cref{thm_ultrafilters_are_compact_right_top}, $\beta S$ is a compact, Hausdorff right-topological semigroup.  By \cref{lemma_exists_minimal_left_ideal}, every left ideal contains a minimal left ideal.  By \cref{lemma_left_ideal_contains_idempotent}, every (minimal) left ideal contains an idempotent.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Ultrafilters as the phase space of an $S$-system}

\begin{theorem}
    \label{thm_ultrafilters_as_system}
    \lean{ultrafilterSystem}
    \leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup.  With left multiplication by elements of $S$ (via principal ultrafilters) and the topology described above, $\beta S$ is an $S$-system.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lemma_left_multiplication_is_continuous}
\leanok{}
    $\beta S$ is a compact Hausdorff space.  The semigroup $S$ acts on $\beta S$ by multiplication on the left, which, by \cref{lemma_left_multiplication_is_continuous}, is continuous.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Subsystems in $\beta S$]
\label{thm_beta_S_subsystem_characterization}
\lean{ultraSubsystemIffClosedLeftIdeal}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup.  The $S$-subsystems of $\beta S$ are precisely the closed left ideals of $\beta S$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\uses{thm_ultra_right_mult_continuous,lemma_image_closure_equals_closure_image}
\leanok{}
    Let $Z$ be an $S$-subsystem of $\beta S$.  Then $SZ \subseteq Z$.  Let $z \in Z$.  By \cref{thm_ultra_right_mult_continuous}, $\rho_z: \beta S \to \beta S$ is continuous and $\rho_z S \subseteq Z$.  By \cref{lemma_image_closure_equals_closure_image},
    \[\rho_z(\beta S) = \rho_z(\overline{S}) = \overline{\rho_z S} \subseteq \overline{Z} = Z,\]
    whereby $(\beta S)z \subseteq Z$.  Since $z$ was arbitrary, we have that $(\beta S)Z \subseteq Z$, meaning that $Z$ is a left ideal.  Since $Z$ is closed, we see that $Z$ is a closed left ideal, as desired.

    Conversely, suppose that $L$ is a closed left ideal.  Since $(\beta S)L \subseteq L$, we have in particular that $SL \subseteq L$.  So, $L$ is non-empty, closed, and $S$-invariant.  Therefore, it is an $S$-subsystem of $\beta S$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Minimal subsystems in $\beta S$]
\label{thm_minimal_beta_S_subsystem_characterization}
\lean{ultraMinSubsystemIffMinLeftIdeal}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup.  The minimal $S$-subsystems of $\beta S$ are precisely the minimal left ideals of $\beta S$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\uses{thm_beta_S_subsystem_characterization,lemma_left_ideal_contains_closed_left_ideal}
\leanok{}
    Let $Z$ be a minimal $S$-subsystem of $\beta S$.  By \cref{thm_beta_S_subsystem_characterization}, $Z$ is a left ideal of $\beta S$.  Let $L \subseteq Z$ be a left ideal of $S$ contained in $Z$.  By \cref{lemma_left_ideal_contains_closed_left_ideal}, there exists a closed left ideal $L'$ of $S$ contained in $L$.  By \cref{thm_beta_S_subsystem_characterization}, $L'$ is an $S$-subsystem of $\beta S$.  Since $Z$ is a minimal $S$-subsystem, $L' = L = Z$.  Therefore, we have shown that $Z$ is a minimal left ideal of $\beta S$.

    Conversely, suppose $L$ is a minimal left ideal of $S$. By \cref{thm_beta_S_subsystem_characterization}, $L$ is an $S$-subsystem of $\beta S$.  To see that it is minimal, suppose $Z \subseteq L$ is an $S$-subsystem of $L$. By \cref{thm_beta_S_subsystem_characterization}, $Z$ is a left ideal of $S$ contained in $L$.  Since $L$ is minimal, $Z = L$, showing that $L$ is a minimal $S$-subsystem of $\beta S$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[{cf. \cite[Lemma 19.6]{hindman_strauss_book_2012}}]
\label{thm_minimal_ultrafilter_iff_unif_rec}
\lean{ultrafilterMinimalIffUnifRec}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup.  An ultrafilter is minimal if and only if it is $S$-uniformly recurrent.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\uses{thm_minimal_beta_S_subsystem_characterization,lemma_pts_in_minimal_are_ur,lemma_orb_closure_of_ur_is_minimal,def_minimal_ultrafilter}
\leanok{}
    Suppose $p \in \beta S$ is minimal.  By definition, it is contained in a minimal left ideal $L$.  By \cref{thm_minimal_beta_S_subsystem_characterization}, $L$ is a minimal $S$-subsystem of $\beta S$. By \cref{lemma_pts_in_minimal_are_ur}, $p$ is $S$-uniformly recurrent.

    Conversely, suppose that $p$ is $S$-uniformly recurrent.  By \cref{lemma_orb_closure_of_ur_is_minimal}, $\overline{Sp}$ is a minimal $S$-subsystem of $\beta S$ containing $p$. By \cref{thm_minimal_beta_S_subsystem_characterization}, $\overline{Sp}$ is a minimal left ideal.  Therefore, $p$ is contained in a minimal left ideal, implying (\cref{def_minimal_ultrafilter}) it is minimal.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Ultrafilters as a semigroup acting on $X$}



In this section, for $f: S \to X$, recall that $\tilde f: \beta S \to X$ is the unique continuous extension of $f$ to $\beta S$.

\begin{definition}[$p-\lim$]
\lean{ultraLim}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a set, $X$ a compact Hausdorff space, and $f: S \to X$.  We write $p-\lim_{s \in S} f(s)$ to mean $\tilde f (p)$.  (Alternative: equivalently, $p-\lim_{s \in S} f(s)$ is the point $x \in X$ with the property that for all non-empty, open $U \ni x$, $\{s \in S : f(s) \in U\} \in p$.)
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[$(pq)-\lim$ description]
\label{thm_pq_lim_is_plim_qlim}
\lean{iteratedUltraLims}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup, $X$ a compact Hausdorff space, and $f: S \to X$.  For all $p, q \in \beta S$,
    \[(pq)-\lim_s f(s) = p-\lim_s (q-\lim_t f(st)).\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lemma_left_multiplication_is_continuous,thm_ultra_right_mult_continuous}
\leanok{}
    \dgg{These steps are broken into separate results in the lean miniproject on this topic.}
    
    Step 1: for $s \in S$ and $f: S \to X$,
    \[\widetilde{f \circ \lambda_s} = \tilde f \circ \lambda_{\delta_s}.\]
    This needs that multiplication on the left by principal ultrafilters is continuous (\cref{lemma_left_multiplication_is_continuous}).

    Step 2: for $q \in \beta S$ and $f: S \to X$, the extension of the map $s \mapsto \widetilde{f \circ \lambda_s}(q)$ to $\beta S$ is $\tilde f \circ \rho_q$. This requires that multiplication on the right is continuous (\cref{thm_ultra_right_mult_continuous}).

    Step 3: For $p, q \in \beta S$ and $f: S \to X$, the extension of the map $s \mapsto \widetilde{f \circ \lambda_s}(q)$ evaluated at $p$ is equal to $\tilde f(pq)$.  That is to say, $(pq)-\lim_s f(s) = p-\lim_s (q-\lim_t f(st))$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Continuous function and $p-\lim$]
\label{thm_continuity_and_plim}
\lean{ultraLimContinuousComp}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup, $X, Y$ compact Hausdorff spaces, $f: S \to X$, and $\pi: X \to Y$ continuous.  For all $p \in \beta S$, $p-\lim_{s \in S} \pi (f (s)) = \pi (p-\lim_{s \in S} f (s))$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok{}
    The statement here is that $\widetilde {\pi \circ f} = \pi \circ \tilde f$.  This is easy to see by checking the equality on the dense set of principal ultrafilters.  Indeed, for $s \in S$, $(\pi \circ f)(s) = \pi(f(s))$.
\end{proof}

Notation: For $p \in \beta S$ and $x \in X$, write $px$ for $p-\lim_{s \in S} sx$.

\begin{theorem}[Ultrafilter semigroup action theorem]
\label{thm_ultra_semigroup_action}
\lean{ultraAction}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup. Let $X$ be an $S$-system.  The map $\beta S \times X \to X$ given by $(p,x) \mapsto p-\lim_{s \in S} sx$ yields an action of $\beta S$ on $X$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\uses{thm_pq_lim_is_plim_qlim,thm_continuity_and_plim}
\leanok{}
    For $p, q \in \beta S$ and $x \in X$, we want to show that 
    \[(pq) x = p(qx).\]
    To see this, we get by \cref{thm_pq_lim_is_plim_qlim} and using that acting on the left by $s$ is a continuous map $X \to X$(\cref{thm_continuity_and_plim}), we see
    \begin{align*}
        (pq) x &= (pq)-\lim_s sx\\
        &= p-\lim_s (q-\lim_t (st)x ) \\
        &= p-\lim_s (q-\lim_t s(tx)) \\
        &= p-\lim_s s(q-\lim_t tx) \\
        &= p-\lim_s s (qx) \\
        &= p(qx).
    \end{align*}
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}
    \label{thm_ultrafilter_action_with_fixed_x_is_continuous}
    \lean{ultraActionWithFixedxIsContinuous}
    \leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup, $X$ be an $S$-system, and $x \in X$.  The map $\beta S \to X$ defined by $p \mapsto px$ is continuous.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok{}
    By definition, the map $\beta S \to X$ defined by $p \mapsto px$ is the lift of the map $S \to X$ defined by $s \mapsto sx$.  Therefore, it is continuous.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}
    \label{thm_ultra_factor_map}
    \lean{ultraFactorMap}
    \leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup, $X$ be an $S$-system, and $x \in X$.  If $\overline{Sx} = X$, then the map $\beta S \to X$ defined by $p \mapsto px$ is a factor map of $S$-systems.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\uses{thm_ultrafilter_action_with_fixed_x_is_continuous}
\leanok{}
    By \cref{thm_ultrafilter_action_with_fixed_x_is_continuous}, the map is continuous.  Since $\overline{Sx} = X$, we have that $p \mapsto px$, the lift of $s \mapsto sx$, is surjective.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}
    \label{thm_ultra_point_in_orb_closure}
    \lean{ultraActionInOrbitClosure}
    \leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup, $X$ be an $S$-system, $x \in X$, and $p \in \beta S$. Then $px \in \overline{Sx}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok{}
    Recall that the map $\beta S \to X$ defined by $p \mapsto px$ is the lift of the map $S \to X$ defined by $s \mapsto sx$.  Fix $x \in X$.  For all $s \in S$, $sx \in \overline{Sx}$.  Therefore, for all $p \in \beta S$, $px \in \overline{Sx}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Ultrafilter action through factors]
\label{thm_ultra_semigroup_action_thru_factor}
\lean{ultraActionIntertwinesWithFactor}
\leanok{}
    Let $X$ and $Y$ be $S$-systems, and let $\pi: X \to Y$ be a factor map.  For all $p \in \beta S$, $p \pi = \pi p$ as maps from $X \to Y$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\uses{thm_continuity_and_plim}
\leanok{}
    Let $p \in \beta S$ and $x \in X$.  By swapping $p-\lim$ and $\pi$ (\cref{thm_continuity_and_plim}), we see that
    \[p \pi x = p-\lim_{s \in S} s \pi x = p-\lim_{s \in S} \pi s x = \pi p-\lim_{s \in S} s x = \pi p x,\]
    as desired.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}
\label{lemma_plim_in_open_set_return_time}
\lean{visitTimeSetBelongsToUltrafilter}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup and $X$ be an $S$-system.  Let $x \in X$, $U \subseteq X$ be open, and $p \in \beta S$.  If $px \in U$, then $R(x,U) \in p$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
    \leanok{}
    This statement follows immediately from the definition of the filter limit.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}
\label{lemma_return_time_implies_plim}
\lean{visitTimeSetInUltraImpliesUltraActInClosure}
\leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup and $X$ be an $S$-system.  Let $x \in X$, $U \subseteq X$, and $p \in \beta S$.  If $R(x,U) \in p$, then $px \in \overline{U}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lemma_plim_in_open_set_return_time}
\leanok{}
    Suppose that $px \not\in \overline{U}$.  Then $px \in X \setminus \overline{U}$. By \cref{lemma_plim_in_open_set_return_time}, $R(x,X \setminus \overline{U}) \in p$.  But $R(x,X \setminus \overline{U}) = S \setminus R(x, \overline{U})$, whereby $R(x, \overline{U}) \not\in p$. Therefore, $R(x,U) \not\in p$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Surjectivity lemma]
\label{lemma_ultrafilter_surjective_action}
\lean{minLeftIdealSurjectsOntoMinSystem}
\leanok{}
Let $X$ be a minimal $S$-system and $L \subseteq \beta S$ be a minimal left ideal.  For all $x, y \in X$, there exists $p \in L$ such that $px = y$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{thm_ultra_factor_map,thm_minimal_beta_S_subsystem_characterization,lemma_img_of_inv_is_inv}
\leanok{}
    Fix $x \in X$. By the minimality of the $S$-action, $\overline{Sx} = X$.  By \cref{thm_ultra_factor_map}, the map $\pi: \beta S \to X$ given by $p \mapsto px$ is a factor map.  By \cref{thm_minimal_beta_S_subsystem_characterization}, $L$ is an $S$-minimal subsystem of $\beta S$.  By \cref{lemma_img_of_inv_is_inv}, $\pi L$ is an $S$-subsystem of $X$.  But $X$ is minimal, so $\pi L = X$.  Therefore, there exists $p \in L$ such that $px = y$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Idempotent ultrafilter lemma]
\label{lemma_exists_idempotent_px_is_x}
\lean{everyPointInMinFixedBySomeIdempotentUltrafilter}
\leanok{}
Let $S$ be a semigroup and $X$ be a minimal $S$-system.  Let $L \subseteq \beta S$ be a minimal left ideal.  For all $x \in X$, there exists an idempotent $p \in L$ such that $px = x$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lemma_ultrafilter_surjective_action,thm_ultrafilter_action_with_fixed_x_is_continuous,lemma_minimal_left_ideal_compact,thm_ultrafilters_are_compact_right_top,thm_ultra_semigroup_action,exists_idempotent_in_compact_subsemigroup}
\leanok{}
    Fix $x \in X$. Let $A = \{p \in \beta S : px = x\}$.  By \cref{lemma_ultrafilter_surjective_action}, $A \cap L$ is non-empty. Since $\{x\}$ is closed and $p \mapsto px$ is continuous (\cref{thm_ultrafilter_action_with_fixed_x_is_continuous}), the set $A$ is closed.  Since $L$ is closed (\cref{lemma_minimal_left_ideal_compact} and \cref{thm_ultrafilters_are_compact_right_top}), $A \cap L$ is closed. By \cref{thm_ultra_semigroup_action}, if $p, q \in A$, then $pq \in A$.  Since $L$ is a left ideal, if $p, q \in L$, then $pq \in L$.  Therefore, $A \cap L$ is a closed, hence compact, subsemigroup of $\beta S$. Apply \cref{exists_idempotent_in_compact_subsemigroup}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Points $x$ and $px$ are proximal]
    \label{lemma_idempotent_implies_proximal}
    \lean{pointAndUltraImageAreProximal}
    \leanok{}
    Let $X$ be an $S$-system, $x \in X$, and $p \in \beta S$ an idempotent ultrafilter.  The points $x$ and $px$ are proximal.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{thm_ultra_semigroup_action,def_proximal}
    \leanok{}
    Let $\alpha \subseteq X^2$ be an entourage.  By the semigroup action theorem (\cref{thm_ultra_semigroup_action}), 
    \[p(x,px) = (px,ppx) = (px,px) \in \Delta \subseteq \alpha.\]
    Therefore, by the definition of proximal (\cref{def_proximal}) $p-\lim$, there exists (many) $s \in S$ such that $(sx,spx) \in \alpha$, as was to be shown.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[$px$ is uniformly recurrent]
    \label{lemma_minimal_implies_uniformly_recurrent}
    \lean{minUltraImageIsUniformlyRecurrent}
    \leanok{}
    Let $S$ be a semigroup, $X$ be an $S$-system, $x \in X$, and $p \in \beta S$ be a minimal ultrafilter.  The point $px$ is $S$-uniformly recurrent.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{thm_ultrafilters_as_system,thm_ultra_semigroup_action,thm_minimal_ultrafilter_iff_unif_rec}
\leanok{}
    Let $U \ni px$.  Choose an open set $V \ni px$ such that $\overline{V} \subseteq U$.  By the definition of $p-\lim$, $p \in \overline{R(x,V)}$.  By \cref{thm_ultrafilters_as_system}, we can treat $\beta S$ as an $S$-system. We claim that
    \[R(p,\overline{R(x,V)}) \subseteq R(px,U).\]
    Indeed, note first that $s \in R(p,\overline{R(x,V)})$ implies $sp \in \overline{R(x,V)}$ implies $R(x,V) \in sp$, which by the definition of $p-\lim$ implies $(sp)x \in \overline{V} \subseteq U$.  By the ultrafilter action theorem (\cref{thm_ultra_semigroup_action}), $(sp)x = s (px)$, so $s(px) \in U$, which implies that $s \in R(px,U)$.

    Since $p$ is minimal, by \cref{thm_minimal_ultrafilter_iff_unif_rec}, $p$ is $S$-uniformly recurrent.  Therefore, $R(p,\overline{R(x,V)})$ is syndetic. Therefore, so is $R(px,U)$, as was to be shown.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Technical idempotent lifting lemma]
\label{lemma_ultrafilter_idempotent_lift}
\lean{idempotentProductLifting}
\leanok{}
    Let $X$ be an $S$-system and $x \in X$.  Let $L \subseteq \beta S$ be a minimal left ideal and $p \in L$.  If $p x = x$, there exists $q \in L$ such that $q x = x$ and $pq$ is idempotent.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{thm_ultrafilters_as_system,thm_minimal_beta_S_subsystem_characterization,lemma_exists_idempotent_px_is_x,lemma_ultrafilter_surjective_action,thm_ultra_semigroup_action,lemma_ru_is_r_for_idempotent}
\leanok{}
    By \cref{thm_ultrafilters_as_system}, we can treat $\beta S$ as an $S$-system. By \cref{thm_minimal_beta_S_subsystem_characterization}, $L$ is a minimal $S$-system. By \cref{lemma_exists_idempotent_px_is_x}, there exists an idempotent $u \in L$ so that $up = p$.  By \cref{lemma_ultrafilter_surjective_action} (twice), there exist $q, r \in L$ such that $qp = u$ and $rq = u$. By the semigroup action of $\beta S$ on $X$ (\cref{thm_ultra_semigroup_action}),
    \[qx = q (p x) = (qp) x = ux = u (px) = (up) x = px = x.\]
    By \cref{lemma_ru_is_r_for_idempotent}, $ru = r$.  Therefore, we also have
    \[pq = (up)q = u (pq) = (rq) (pq) = r (qp) q = ruq = (ru) q = rq = u,\]
    as desired.
\end{proof}

% Let $X$ be a $G$-system. Then we have:

%     \item for a fixed $x \in X$, a continuous map $\beta G \to X$ given by $p \mapsto px$ (that is, the action map $\beta G \times X \to X$ given by $(p,x) \mapsto px$ is continuous in the left coordinate).  When $x$ is a transitive point of $X$, this map is a surjection, and hence is a factor map of $G$-systems;

%     \item for all minimal $p \in \beta G$ and $x \in X$, the point $px$ is uniformly recurrent;

%     \item for all idempotent $p \in \beta G$ and $x \in X$, the points $x$ and $px$ are proximal; and, conversely, if the points $x$ and $y$ are proximal and if $y$ is in the orbit-closure of $x$, then there exists an idempotent $p \in \beta G$ such that $px = y$.

For $p \in \beta S$, we push $p$ forward using \verb|Ultrafilter.map| through this map to get an ultrafilter on $X$.  Then, we take the ultrafilter limit on $X$.  Ultrafilter limits exist by compactness and \verb|IsCompact.ultrafilter_le_nhds|
